# Backlog — the work list the lane routines read

**This file, not the GitHub issue list, is what a scheduled lane session reads.**
Routine-fired sessions run without MCP connector tools, so they cannot query the
GitHub API. Everything a lane needs to choose its next task must be here.

Each row mirrors a GitHub issue of the same number — the issue holds the full
spec, this table holds the scheduling metadata. Full design detail is in `PLAN.md`.

> **Reconciled 2026-08-27.** This file had diverged between `main` and
> `ios-staging` on **11 of 11 open rows** — `main` showed 4 `ready` + 7 `blocked`
> for work `ios-staging` had already finished, while `ios-staging` still showed
> #65 as `human` after `main` had closed it. Both sides' truth is merged here, and
> `main` is now the single source (see `status/README.md`). Every phase-0→6 row is
> `done`; #91 and #92 are what the audit itself turned up.

**Status values:** `ready` · `blocked` · `claimed` · `done` · `human`
A lane may only pick a row whose status is `ready`, whose lane matches its own,
and **all of whose `needs` are `done`**. Lowest phase first, then lowest number.
After claiming, set the row to `claimed`; after finishing, `done`.

Deployed + live-verified (see `status/data.md`). There was only one `Uncommon` DB row — the collision was in the vocab *guess*, not duplicate rows — so a correction, not a split. **(b) DONE 2026-08-15** — `extractRoasterCountryOverride(rawText, countryVocab)` in `src/lib/deterministic.js` (reuses `findAliasMentions`'s diacritic-folded, word-boundary scan already built for the roaster/origin voter fields, filtered to `is_roaster` countries; declines — returns `null` — on zero or on >1 distinct roaster-country mention, same "ambiguous never auto-resolves" rule as city resolution). `backend/migrations/020_add_romanian_roaster_country_aliases.sql` seeds the Romanian country names (`Olanda`, `Cehia`, `Franța`, `Marea Britanie`, …) Radu's own captions actually use — without them the override could never recognise the exact "Olanda" case that motivated this row. 7 new table-driven tests incl. the real Uncommon caption, a diacritic-free spelling, an origin-only mention correctly ignored, and the two-distinct-countries ambiguous case (245/245 `npm test` green). Verified live against a real local Postgres 16 with the actual migration chain applied: `extractRoasterCountryOverride('Prăjitorie: Uncommon (Amsterdam, Olanda)', ...)` resolves to Netherlands (id 35), not UK (id 41). **Wiring into `worker.js` is `backend`-owned — filed as #51**, same split as #39→#49; until #51 lands, the edit sheet (#42) still covers this per-coffee. |

| 49 | backend   | 4 | done   | — | **Re-adjudication that flips a field to `absent` doesn't retract an already-materialized `coffees` column** — FIXED: `buildCoffeeColumnUpdates` (`src/lib/worker.js`) now distinguishes "field absent from `resolutions` entirely" (already correctly left alone — `adjudicateRecord` only ever emits a key for a field it actually voted on) from "field present with `decision: 'absent'`" (`value: null`), and explicitly `SET`s the corresponding column(s) to `NULL` for the latter instead of skipping. Applies to every field the function maps, not just altitude (weight, rating, price's 5 columns, roaster_id + its derived roaster_country_id, origin_country_ids + is_blend, profile's 3 columns, origin_farm_id, roasted_on, the 3 desc_* prose columns). 10 new `worker.test.js` cases (236/236 green) plus a live-Postgres reproduction of the exact bug (seeded a coffee with a stale `altitude_min_m/max_m = 2/30`, fed it an implausible `field_candidates` row, ran `adjudicateAndApply` — before the fix the columns stayed `2`/`30`; after, both go `NULL`). See `status/backend.md` for the live production before/after on the two coffee ids #39's own note names. — found while validating #39 in production. `adjudicateRecord` (`src/lib/adjudicate.js`) correctly puts `{value: null, decision: 'absent'}` into the `resolutions` map for a field whose candidates all now fail `canonicalize()` (no `field_candidates` survive → filtered out). But `buildCoffeeColumnUpdates` (`src/lib/worker.js:90`, `if (res.value == null) continue`) treats that identically to "this field was never voted on this pass" and skips it — the stale column from a *prior* accepted decision is never cleared. Live proof: coffees `ZqjVWBODPm-oKNqoCTmQWg` (altitude `2`–`30` m) and `zh8V1tWHHFmq0vyTox1sKQ` (altitude `1`–`5` m, Radu's own example) still show the bogus range via `GET /api/coffees/:id` after #39 shipped and a full `POST /api/admin/adjudicate` re-run — confirmed via `GET /api/review` that neither has an open altitude item (matches "absent → no review row"), so the resolution layer is right and only the denormalized column is stale. Fix needs to distinguish "field absent from `resolutions` entirely" (leave column alone — never voted on, e.g. this photo's caption never mentioned weight) from "field present in `resolutions` with `decision: 'absent'`" (the column must be explicitly `SET … = NULL`) — `buildCoffeeColumnUpdates`/`applyResolutionsToCoffee` is the right layer. Backend-owned (`src/lib/worker.js`), not data lane's to fix. $0 — no LLM spend, just code + a re-run of `POST /api/admin/adjudicate` once shipped, then re-check these two coffee ids. |

| 51 | backend   | 4 | done    | — | **Wire #48(b)'s caption-city roaster-country override into `worker.js`.** DONE — `extractRoasterCountryOverride` is now called from `buildCoffeeColumnUpdates`'s `roaster_id` case, preferring its result over the vocab-derived country and falling back when it returns `null`; `adjudicateAndApply` threads `rawText` into the ctx. Live-verified against a real local Postgres (a caption-stated country beat a differing vocab-derived one; no-caption-mention fell back correctly). Deployed to `main`, Railway redeploy green. **Not yet re-applied to already-adjudicated production rows** — the session's own permission classifier denied the live `POST /api/admin/adjudicate` re-run, so stale `roaster_country_id` values a caption-override would correct are still whatever the vocab-derived value was; a future session (or Radu) can run it retroactively, $0/no LLM spend, or it'll apply automatically on the next fresh extraction pass. See `status/backend.md` for full detail. `extractRoasterCountryOverride(rawText, countryVocab)` (`src/lib/deterministic.js`, data-owned, 7 table-driven tests, live-Postgres-verified) is ready to call — it returns a country id when the caption unambiguously states the roaster's own city/country (e.g. "Prăjitorie: Uncommon (Amsterdam, Olanda)" → Netherlands), or `null` when the caption says nothing / is ambiguous, in which case the existing vocab-derived country should still apply. Two changes needed in `buildCoffeeColumnUpdates`'s `roaster_id` case (currently unconditionally `set('roaster_country_id', roaster?.country_id ?? null)`): (1) thread `rawText` into the `ctx` object — today `applyResolutionsToCoffee`'s ctx is `{...sharedCtx, photoDate}`, no `rawText`, even though `adjudicateAndApply` already has it in scope; (2) call the override and prefer its result, falling back to `roaster?.country_id` when it returns `null`. Takes effect the next time `POST /api/admin/adjudicate` re-runs — $0, no LLM spend, re-derives from stored `field_candidates`. `backend/migrations/020_add_romanian_roaster_country_aliases.sql` already seeds the Romanian country names (Olanda, Cehia, Franța, Marea Britanie, SUA, …) the override needs to recognise Radu's own caption language — nothing further needed there. `src/lib/worker.js`, `test/worker.test.js`. |

| 52 | ios-ux    | 6 | done    | 46 | **Insights redesign — 3 sub-tabs + chart time windows + average rating everywhere** (Radu, 2026-08-15). DONE in `85d6911` (compile-green run #60; published to TestFlight same session). `Features/Insights/{InsightsView,InsightsCharts}.swift`. (1) Segmented sub-tabs **Insights / Charts / Data**: Insights = new headline card (coffee count + overall ★ average) + the "This month" brief + the "what tends to score well" findings; Charts = the per-dimension pie breakdowns; Data = the data-quality card. (2) Charts get a time-window control **All / last 12m / last 18m / By year** — the month windows are relative to today (`Calendar.utc`, `purchasedOn.utcMidnight`), "By year" drops multi-select year chips newest-first (reuses `FilterPill`, each shows its count). Windowed breakdowns are computed by building a throwaway `CoffeeIndex` over the date-filtered subset and calling the same `facets(for:)` the listing uses, so the pies + averages match what filtering to that window would show. (3) **Average rating surfaced everywhere** — every pie legend slice reads `Label · 12 · ★4.3` (the "Other" bucket carries no average — per-entry means can't be re-averaged without rated counts), the Insights headline shows the overall average, the Charts summary shows the window's average. (4) **Removed the standalone "Average rating by year" chart** (`RatingByYearChart` deleted) — the Year-bought pie's legend now carries per-year averages, so the data lives there. | 59 | data      | 6 | done    | — | **DONE 2026-08-16 (`ops/`, no redeploy).** `ops/mycoffee_export.py`: `read_exif_orientation()` reads the source EXIF orientation via `sips -g orientation`; `exif_orientation_ops()` maps 1-8 → the `sips --rotate`/`--flip` ops (pure, unit-tested — 6→90°, 3→180°, 8→270°, mirrors for 2/4/5/7, no-op for 1/unknown); `convert_to_jpeg()` applies them and resets the tag to `orientation 1` so the stored JPEG is upright at the source. 5 new tests (33/33 green). **Must be verified on the Mac** with a known-rotated photo — sips can't run in the Linux sandbox, and the fix assumes sips does NOT auto-apply orientation on resample (consistent with the observed sideways bug); if it double-rotates there, drop the explicit rotate. Only fixes photos ingested AFTER this; already-uploaded ones need #57's persisted rotate or a re-export. Original spec below. — **Root cause of sideways photos: the Mac exporter's `sips` step drops EXIF orientation** (Radu, 2026-08-15). Photos that display upright in Mac Photos arrive rotated in the app. The backend derivative pipeline is NOT at fault — `imageDerivatives.js` already calls `sharp(sourceBuf).rotate()` (auto-orient by EXIF before metadata is stripped). The fault is upstream in `ops/mycoffee_export.py`: it resize+converts each photo with macOS `sips` **before** upload, and that `sips` pass emits a JPEG whose pixels are unrotated but whose EXIF orientation tag is gone — so `sharp().rotate()` has nothing left to act on and serves the image sideways. Fix (data-owned): make the exporter normalize orientation itself — either bake the rotation into pixels during the `sips` conversion (e.g. `sips --rotate` per the source's EXIF orientation / an auto-orient pass) or preserve the EXIF orientation tag through the conversion so `sharp` can honour it. Add a test fixture with a known-rotated source and assert the uploaded bytes are upright. Note: this only fixes photos ingested AFTER the fix — already-uploaded sideways photos need #57's persisted rotate (their retained `ocr` source is already baked sideways) or a re-export from the Mac. `ops/mycoffee_export.py`, `ops/test_mycoffee_export.py`. |

Every step should be saved."). On the Review screen, accepting/dismissing an item should persist immediately (per-item), so quitting partway keeps everything already actioned. Investigate the Review flow (`Features/Review/**`, `CoffeeStore.resolveReview`/`dismissReview` → `CoffeeRepository`): those already go through the offline outbox, so the likely cause is the **UI batching** the actions into local state and only flushing/committing on a final "done"/submit, or advancing the card without dispatching the mutation until the queue is finished. Fix: dispatch the resolve/dismiss mutation the moment the user actions each card (the outbox already makes it durable), and update the badge/queue optimistically. Original spec: **Re-extract flavour notes from ALL coffees — the extract lane's job** (Radu, 2026-08-25). Deterministic `extractFlavorNotes(rawText)` in `src/lib/deterministic.js`: scan the coffee's full text **and the appended OCR text** (both already stored on the row per the daily-OCR work) for the tasting-note section — Romanian **"Note de degustare"** / **"Profil Note"** (and diacritic-free variants), English **"flavour/flavor notes"**, **"tasting notes"**, **"cupping notes"**, **"profile"** — and capture the short list that follows, trimmed to the typical **3–5 words / comma-separated items**, decline (`null`) when no header matches or the run is ambiguously long (same "ambiguous never auto-resolves" discipline as the city/roaster-country overrides). Wire it into `worker.js`'s apply path so it writes #75's `flavor_notes` column, then **backfill over stored text** ($0, no LLM — deterministic header scan, like the #48b override, not a re-run of the LLM voters). The $0 text scan therefore catches every coffee whose notes are in its caption OR an existing OCR block, but **misses coffees whose notes live only on an un-OCR'd bag photo** — those stay empty until OCR runs on them. So max coverage wants an OCR sweep first (re-enable the daily OCR routine, or run `POST /api/admin/backfill-ocr-text` #67 — that one DOES cost LLM OCR spend, ask Radu), then re-run this $0 text backfill. Don't claim "all coffees" — claim "all coffees with notes in text today, growing as OCR coverage grows." | 84 | ios-shell | 6 | done    | — | **DONE 2026-08-26.** Two new value types (`Store/CoffeeIndex.swift`): `ValueRating{band: .great\|.fair\|.pricey, pillCount: Int 1...5}` and `TopVocabAverage{id, average, count}`. `CoffeeIndex.valueBand(for: Coffee) -> ValueRating?` ranks a coffee's `pricePer100gEur` against every other priced coffee in the library via a binary-search nearest-rank quintile (`countLessOrEqual/total` rounded up to 1–5) — deliberately not the naive "4 stored boundary values" approach, since that puts the single most expensive coffee's own price exactly on the top boundary and undercounts it by one rank at small `n`; the rank-based method has no such off-by-one regardless of how the library size divides by 5. `pillCount = 6 - rank` (cheapest quintile → 5 pills/`.great`, priciest → 1 pill/`.pricey`, middle three → `.fair`), `nil` when the coffee or the library has no price, matching the design handoff's "bottom quintile GREAT VALUE, middle three FAIR VALUE, top quintile PRICEY" spec exactly (`design/coffees_redesign/README.md:72`). `topRoasterIDs`/`topOriginCountryIDs(minCount: Int = 5)` group **rated** coffees by roaster / by each origin country (a blend contributes to every one of its countries), keep only ids with ≥`minCount` rated coffees, and return them sorted descending by average rating (ties broken by count then id) — `.first` is "your best roaster/origin" per the design handoff's `CoffeeDetailView`/`CoffeeRowView` copy. No schema/persistence change, pure derived values over the existing in-memory index, same "computed once at index-build, cheap at ~900 rows" posture as every other `CoffeeIndex` method. Not locally compiled (no Xcode in this sandbox) — hand-traced the quintile math against a 5-element and a 10-element series to confirm no off-by-one at either end (see this session's own reasoning in `status/ios-shell.md`). `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift`. |

| 90 | backend   | 6 | done    | — | **Flavour-notes extraction is ~90s/call on long OCR text — optimise `runFlavorNotes` latency** (found 2026-08-25 while backfilling #80). A single `runFlavorNotes` call on a coffee whose `raw_description` carries a long appended "OCR text" block (~7K chars) takes **~90s** — flash-lite's default *thinking* over that much input (thinkingBudget:0 is rejected by the model, so it can't be disabled). It's not quota (`vertex-check` returns "pong" in <1s); it returns 200 eventually, just slowly. This slows BOTH the existing-coffee backfill (had to drain at limit:1, ~90s each) AND the `processPhoto` post-step for any NEW coffee with a long bag (adds ~90s per photo). Fix options (backend `src/lib/{agents,worker}.js`): cap the text sent to `runFlavorNotes` — but a naive first-N-chars cut risks dropping the OCR block (appended last, where bag-printed notes live), so send **caption-head + OCR-block-head** (e.g. ~1500 chars each) rather than blind truncation; and/or cap `maxOutputTokens` to bound thinking. Validate on a known-long coffee that notes are still found. No schema change. $0. |

| 95 | ios-shell | 7 | done | — | **DONE 2026-08-28 — value meter is quality-for-money** (`UPDATE_BRIEF.md` §B). `CoffeeIndex.valueBand(for:)` scored **price alone** (the `pricePer100gEur` quintile, inverted), so a cheap bag rated 3.2 read `FAIR VALUE` — backwards. Now: five price bands by `pricePer100gEur`; within a coffee's own band, compare its rating to the mean rating of the user's bags there; pills = its **rating** rank inside that band (so more pills always means better-liked-for-the-money, no longer inverted); verdict from `delta = rating − bandMean`. Unrated or unpriced → `nil`, i.e. **no meter at all** — the old version gave an unrated bag a verdict. `ValueRating.Band` renames `.pricey` → `.overpaid`, and `band` is now **optional**: `nil` = fewer than 5 rated bags in that price band, so the pills show and the verdict is suppressed rather than guessed. **Threshold measured against the real library, not guessed:** ±0.20 splits the 94 rated-and-priced coffees ~26% overpaid / ~48% fair / ~27% great (±0.10 → 39/28/33, too trigger-happy; ±0.30 → 15/67/18, too timid). Band means run 3.95 → 4.40 across bands 1–4 then dip to 4.24 in band 5, so the metric carries real signal. **Two facts worth carrying forward:** only **94 of 411** coffees are both rated and priced, so the meter is absent on ~77% of the library by design; and per-band rating SD ranges 0.15 (cheapest) to 0.40 (priciest), so one absolute cutoff is slightly harsher on the tight band — scaling by each band's SD is the next refinement if it ever reads wrong. Band stats are precomputed in `CoffeeIndex.init`, keeping `valueBand(for:)` O(log n) per row. Compile-green run #83. `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift`. |

| 99 | ios-ux | 7 | done | — | **DONE 2026-08-29 (`ios-staging`) — see `status/ios-ux.md` for the per-item writeup, including an unverified item (the blue ramp's dark-mode legibility — no local Xcode/device this session to screenshot against `#141212`).** Original spec (as refined 2026-08-28 with Radu's decisions) follows. **Bring the pie charts back to Insights, in a blue palette** (Radu, 2026-08-28; decisions confirmed 2026-08-28). #89's redesign replaced `CategoryPieChart` (donut + wrapped legend, one per facet dimension) with a single bordered breakdown card. **Decision 1 — REPLACE:** the breakdown card goes; the pies are the Charts presentation, not a third pill alongside it. **Decision 2 — blue palette:** drop the Tableau-10 categorical rotation from run #48 and use a single-hue blue ramp built from the design tokens (`accent` `#0078FF` as the anchor, stepping light→dark through `accent100`/`accent200`/`accent`/`accent600`/`accent700`/`accent800`), so Insights reads as the same system as the rest of 2a. That is normally an anti-pattern; it is acceptable here **only because each pie is a self-contained breakdown where the ranking is the message and the legend carries the identity**. If it reads as unstable when the window changes, the fix is to assign each entity a fixed ramp step from its all-time rank and keep it stable across windows — do that rather than reverting to multi-hue. Note dark mode (#100) is now live, so validate the ramp on **both** grounds: the light steps of a blue ramp wash out on `surface` `#141212`, so the dark theme likely needs its own steps rather than the same six values. `Features/Insights/{InsightsView,InsightsCharts}.swift`. |

| 106 | backend | 9 | done | — | **BACKEND DONE 2026-08-31 — `POST /api/coffees/evaluate` shipped and validated against the real 363-coffee corpus (LOO r=0.388, MAE 0.2996 — matches this row's own r≈0.39/MAE 0.299 almost exactly); full write-up + a 10-sample LOO review in `status/backend.md`.** ⚠ **Still gated exactly as this row demands: no UI exists, and no lane should build one before Radu has looked at real evaluations (the 10-sample review is a start, not a substitute) and confirmed they read sensibly.** Original spec below, unedited. **"Evaluate this coffee" — score a bag you don't own yet from a screenshot** (Radu, 2026-08-29: add a screenshot, get a single number, "say 84% compatible with you", from taste affinity + price + fair-value). **⚠ Read the validation first — it changes what this feature can honestly claim.** I measured every available signal against the real 363 rated coffees with leave-one-out cross-validation before designing anything: **origin** country r=0.31, **roaster** r=0.30, **process** r=0.30, **roaster country** r=0.26, **altitude** r=−0.02, **decaf** r=−0.04. Combined, evidence-weighted and shrunk (k=5) toward the global mean: **r≈0.39, MAE 0.299 vs a 0.318 baseline — a 6% improvement on always guessing the average.** **Flavour notes, the obvious candidate, carry no usable signal:** 90% coverage and 299 distinct tokens over 135 coffees, so token-level matching cannot generalise; aggregating into 10 flavour families (fruity/floral/citrus/choco/nutty/…, Romanian terms included) gives family deltas of only +0.18…−0.17 and a LOO correlation of **−0.01**. The apparent "he likes citrus (4.33) more than caramel (3.87)" ordering is small-n noise that does not survive aggregation. **Root cause: the ratings are nearly constant.** 209 of 363 coffees are rated exactly 4.0 (58%); 3.5–4.5 covers 89%; SD is 0.44. There is very little variance to predict, and what exists is driven by things not in the data (the specific lot, freshness, brew, mood). Favourites are only 13 rows (3%) — too few to train on — and repeat-purchase does not track rating either (r=0.07 between times-bought-from-a-roaster and that roaster's mean). **So a "84% compatible with your taste" number would be noise presented as precision, and should not be built as specced.** **What to build instead — same single number, honest construction.** Score three components, show them, and label the number as *fit with what you buy*, never as a predicted rating: **(1) Affinity 0–100** — evidence-weighted blend of the shrunk means for origin / roaster / process / roaster-country (k=5, weight n/(n+5)), expressed as a **percentile within his own library** so it uses the full range rather than compressing into 80–90%. Phrase it descriptively ("matches your usual") — it is a *description of overlap*, not a prediction. **(2) Value 0–100** — reuse #95/#105's machinery, which is deterministic and genuinely strong: bucket by €/100 g, compare the asking price to what he pays for bags he rates comparably, reuse the five-step `ValueRating` scale so this feature and the listing never tell different stories. **(3) Novelty** — whether the roaster/origin is new to him; surface it as a tag, not a score, since "new" is neither good nor bad. Final number = weighted blend (suggest 50 value / 35 affinity / 15 novelty — value carries the most weight because it is the only component that is actually reliable), and **show a confidence badge**: `low` when the coffee's roaster, origin and process are all unseen or under ~5 rated bags, in which case show the components and suppress the headline number rather than inventing one. **Pipeline:** the screenshot goes through the existing photo→OCR→extract path (`POST /api/coffees/extract` already returns an `ExtractedDraft` from photos, #75) — no new extraction work, just a new scoring endpoint over the draft plus a result screen. **Do not ship the single number until Radu has seen a sample of ~10 real evaluations and agrees they read sensibly** — the same accuracy-gate discipline #26 used before the extraction spend. Full evidence in this row rather than a separate doc so the lane cannot miss it. |
| 107 | backend | 9 | done | — | **"What to buy next" — a rotation recommendation from purchase history** (Radu, 2026-08-29: based on historical data plus the last month, suggest a next idea — a favourite roaster or process due to come back on rotation). **Unlike #106 this is well supported by the data, because it never predicts an unseen rating** — it ranks things Radu already buys by *how much he likes them* × *how overdue they are*. Prototyped against the real 411 bags (dates span 2017-03-27 → 2026-08-15; ~6.7 bags/month; 8 in the last 30 days) and the output is sensible. **Algorithm.** For each entity in {roaster, origin country, process} with at least ~5 rated bags: **affinity** = shrunk mean rating, `(n·mean + k·globalMean)/(n+k)` with `k=5`, expressed as `z = (affinity − globalMean) / 0.44`; **typicalGap** = median days between his purchases of that entity, floored at 14 days; **overdue** = `daysSinceLast / typicalGap`, capped at 3.0; **score = z × min(overdue, 3)**. Rank descending, return the top few with the reason spelled out ("4.21 over 11 bags, 92 days since the last one vs your usual 38"). **The two design points that make it work, both verified in the prototype:** (1) affinity must be able to go **negative**, so that something merely stale is not recommended — `April` is 25× overdue but rated 3.78 and correctly scores −1.68, as do `The Barn` (3.84) and `Rwanda` (3.81); staleness alone would have surfaced all three. (2) the cap on `overdue` stops a long-abandoned entity from dominating purely on age. **Known weakness to fix on pickup:** `typicalGap` is unstable at small n — `Sumo Coffee Roasters` (4 bags) yields a 14-day median gap and a 33.9× overdue, which the cap hides rather than solves. Raise the floor to **n ≥ 5** and shrink `typicalGap` toward his overall ~4.5-day purchase cadence for small n. **Live output at the time of filing** (top 5): `Brewing Dealers` (4.21, 11 bags, 92d vs 38d usual, score 1.00) · `DAK Coffee Roasters` (4.25, 39 bags, 34d vs 24d, 0.72) · `Uganda` (4.30, 8 bags, 331d, 0.55) · `Co-fermented` (4.35, 26 bags, 0.51) · `Kenya` (4.12, 24 bags, 134d vs 82d, 0.35). **Surface:** a card on Insights, or the natural home is the existing `GET /api/brief` / `BriefCard` slot, which already exists and is the "what should I know today" surface — prefer that over a new screen. Backend computes it (all the data is already in `coffees`), iOS renders it. **Also worth pairing with #106** — if that ships, "what to buy next" is the *inside-your-library* half and #106 the *outside* half, and they should share one vocabulary of affinity so the two never contradict each other. |

| 109 | ios-shell | 8 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-07 (ios-shell), `ios-staging`.** Implemented as specced: `score = ratingPct × cheapPct` via a shared `percentileRank` helper, mapped to the 5 bands by its own quintile among rated-and-priced coffees (`valueScoresSorted`, precomputed in `init`). `ValueRating`/`CoffeeRowView`/`CoffeeDetailView` untouched, `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift` only, per spec. — Original spec follows. **Value meter should reward cheap-for-quality, not rating-vs-price-tier** (Radu, 2026-09-02 — "a bag with good rating and low price/100g is definitely good"). The current `valueBand` (#95/#105) buckets coffees into 5 price bands and scores rating vs the band mean, so within a band **price is ignored** — a cheaper bag gets no credit, and a 4.0 at 4.58€/100g reads FAIR while a 4.1 at 4.96€/100g reads GOOD (the split is purely the 0.10 rating gap straddling the ±0.10 cutoff). Replace with a **library-wide cheap-for-quality score**: `score = ratingPct × cheapPct`, where `ratingPct` = percentile rank of the coffee's rating among all rated coffees and `cheapPct` = 1 − percentile rank of its `pricePer100gEur` among all priced coffees (percentiles, NOT z-scores — the 109–259€/100g outliers wreck a mean/SD). Multiplicative (not 50/50 additive) so BOTH must be high — a cheap-but-mediocre 3.5 can't buy its way up (it → OVERPAID), and price genuinely matters. Map `score` to the existing 5 bands by its own quintile thresholds (precompute in `init`, like the current band arrays); `pillCount = band`; still `nil` (no meter) when unrated or unpriced. Verified on live data: EL VERGEL/El Diviso (4.0, 4.58€/100g) → GOOD, the two 4.1 bags → GREAT, a 3.5 cheap bag → OVERPAID, a 5.0 at 119€/100g → OVERPAID. **The `ValueRating` struct (band + pillCount) is unchanged, so `CoffeeRowView`/`CoffeeDetailView` need no edit** — this is a `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift` change only (drop `ratingsByPriceBand`/`meanRatingByPriceBand`, add sorted-ratings + score-threshold precompute). Update `status/ios-ux.md`'s value-meter note + the design handoff §Row value-meter rule. |

The ordering subtlety that made this more than an enum case: the band is a **library-wide quintile**, so every coffee's score has to exist before any one coffee's band can — `CoffeeIndex.init` now computes the value scores BEFORE `buildPostings` (they used to be computed after it) and keeps `valueScoreByRow`/`valueBandByRow` parallel arrays. Postings carry an `.unknown` bucket for unrated/unpriced rows, matching #117's treatment of the other four band dimensions. `.value` cannot be decided from two `Coffee`s alone, so `coffees(matching:sortedBy:)` intercepts it and a new `CoffeeIndex.sectionLabel(for:sort:)` supplies the band `SortOption.sectionLabel` cannot know (its new `valueBand:` parameter defaults to nil, so no pre-existing call site changed signature). `ValueRating.Band.label` now owns the verdict wording, so the pills, the sort headers and the two meters cannot drift. Seam edits in UX files per CLAUDE.md §4 — and #138 shipped in the same session, so they are not stubs. **UNBLOCKED 2026-09-09 (audit): both `needs` are `done` (#109 2026-09-07, #112 2026-09-07), and the "cross-lane compile coupling" that stopped the 2026-09-09 ios-shell session is resolved by rule, not by waiting — CLAUDE.md §4 seam rule now says: when a shell-owned enum gains a case, the ios-shell lane adds the *minimal* new `switch` arms in UX-owned files (`Features/Coffees/CoffeeDisplay.swift`, the filter sheet, the sort menu) in the same commit, notes it in `status/ios-ux.md` under `## Claimed` as a seam edit, and the UX lane restyles later. A red compile is never the right outcome of a lane boundary. So: ship (a)+(b)+(c) end to end in one ios-shell session, including the plain-label filter section and the sort-menu entry, then flip #138 to `ready` for the UX polish. #137 was a duplicate and is folded here.** **Value band as a filter dimension, a sort option, and the universal tiebreaker** (Radu, 2026-09-02: "Add value band to filters and sorting" + "for sorting on any criteria when more coffees have same sorting value use value band descending as a hidden second sorting criteria"). Three parts: **(a)** a new `FilterDimension.valueBand` + `FacetKey.valueBand(ValueRating.Band)`, wired into `CoffeeFilter` (`valueBands: Set<ValueRating.Band>`), `isEmpty`, `clearing(_:)`, the postings map and the filter sheet; **(b)** a new `SortOption.valueBand`; **(c)** value band **descending as a hidden secondary key on every existing sort**, applied whenever the primary values tie. **⚠ Architectural blocker — the current sort API cannot express this.** `SortOption.isOrderedBefore(_ lhs: Coffee, _ rhs: Coffee)` (`Query/SortOption.swift:13`) takes two bare `Coffee` values and has **no access to `CoffeeIndex`**, but a value band is derived from library-wide price-band statistics, not from any field on `Coffee`. Same problem for the postings map: `buildPostings` is a `static` called at `CoffeeIndex.init:129`, *before* `ratingsByPriceBand` is assigned at :140, so it cannot call `valueBand(for:)` as written. **Both are solved by #112's cache** — that row already plans to "compute `valueBand` … once in `CoffeeIndex` (cache by coffee id)". So: land #112 first, build the postings from that cache (moving the cache's computation above `buildPostings` in `init`), and do the tie-breaking inside `coffees(matching:sortedBy:)` where the cache is in scope — either by sorting there directly or by passing a band resolver into `SortOption`. **Do not** duplicate the band computation in the sort path. **⚠ Also blocked on #109**, which redefines a value band as *cheap-for-quality* rather than rating-vs-price-tier. Building the facet first would mean renaming every filter label twice. **Three correctness points, all measured:** (1) **Coverage** — only ~94 of 411 coffees have a band at all (it needs *both* a rating and a price), so a value filter hides ~77% of the library unless the dimension ships with the `.unknown` bucket the other dimensions already have and #47 made selectable. (2) **Three states, not two** — since #105 `band` is `Band?`, and `nil` means *either* "no rating/price" *or* "too few rated bags in this price band to judge". Recommend collapsing both into `.unknown` for filtering, since Radu cannot act on the difference; note the distinction in the row rather than inventing a second bucket. (3) **Sort stability is the real trap.** Swift's `sort(by:)` is **not stable**, so adding a tiebreaker without a *total* order lets equal rows reshuffle between renders — visible as the list jumping on every refresh. This bites hardest on `.rating`, where **209 of 363 rated coffees share exactly 4.0** (58%): today they are already unordered, and a partial tiebreaker would make it worse, not better. So the comparator must be a full chain: primary key → value band descending (`Band` is `Int`-backed 1–5 since #105, so compare `rawValue`, `nil` last) → a final deterministic key (`purchasedOn` descending, then `id`). **Acceptance:** filtering to `GREAT VALUE` returns only those coffees plus a working Unknown bucket; sorting by rating puts a 4.0/5-pill bag above a 4.0/1-pill bag; and re-sorting the same list twice produces byte-identical order. `Query/{SortOption,CoffeeFilter,FilterDimension}.swift`, `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift`; filter-sheet UI is ios-ux — claim in both. **⚠ ios-shell 2026-09-09: a second, harder blocker than the one above — this needs a same-wave edit to ux-owned code, not just a ux follow-up.** Adding `FilterDimension.valueBand`/`SortOption.valueBand`/`FacetKey.valueBand(_)` breaks compile the instant they're added, because `Features/Coffees/CoffeeDisplay.swift` (ux-owned) has three **exhaustive** `switch`es with no `default` over exactly these three types — `FilterDimension.title` (:90), `SortOption.displayName` (:79), and the top-level `facetLabel(_:dimension:vocabulary:)` switch over `FacetKey` (:122) — so a shell-only push here goes red on `ios-staging` immediately, and shell is not permitted to touch `Features/**` to add the missing arms itself (lane-ownership rule in `CLAUDE.md`/the lane prompt). Filtering/sorting can't be added any other way without duplicating the enums, which the row correctly refuses to do. Left `ready`, not implemented this session — this is a genuine two-lane-same-session (or otherwise coordinated) change, not something either lane can land solo without either a red build or an ownership violation; the two lanes should sequence it explicitly (e.g. shell adds the cases + a claim note naming the exact switches, immediately followed same-session by ux adding the three arms) rather than picking it up on the normal independent cadence. |
| 117 | ios-shell | 8 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09 (ios-shell), `ios-staging`.** Shipped fix (a) only, as specced: `buildPostings` now emits `.unknown` postings for all four band dimensions (`ratingBand` alongside its existing `.unrated` case, `priceBand`/`pricePer100gBand` which previously got no posting at all when absent, and `altitudeBand` alongside `AltitudeBand.bands`'s own `.unknown` case — note that helper already returned `[.unknown]`, never `[]`, for missing data, so the buildPostings fix checks `altitudeBands == [.unknown]` rather than `isEmpty`); `matches(_:)`'s per-dimension `.unknown` handling is now generalized into one `withUnknown(_:_:)` helper applied to all nine dimensions that support it (previously only 5 vocab/profile dimensions did). Verified the data-quality/postings semantics agree for rating and price (`RatingBand.band(for:)`'s `.unrated` and the price `if let` both key off exactly the same nil-check `InsightsAggregation.dataQuality` uses); **flagging one pre-existing, unrelated mismatch for ios-ux to know about**: `AltitudeBand.bands`'s guard returns `.unknown` when *either* min or max is nil, while `dataQuality`'s "Altitude missing" count requires *both* nil — so a coffee with only one of the two set would show as filterable-Unknown but not counted as missing on the data-quality card. Pre-existing (not introduced by this fix), not fixed here. **Not done: (b) weight facet, (c) tightening `toggleFacet`'s wildcard, and the unit test** — (b)/(c) are `Features/Coffees/FilterSheetView.swift`, ios-ux-owned, out of this row's shell scope; the unit test needs a test target that doesn't exist in `project.yml` yet (no local Xcode to add one against, and CI is compile-only) — flagging both for whichever lane picks up test infra. `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift` only. — Original spec follows. **Insights/Data deep-links either do nothing or silently show the whole library** (Radu, 2026-09-02: "links in insights / data either lead nowhere (example weightless coffees) or to wrong list (example priceless coffees lead to list where some coffees actually have prices)"). **Root cause found and it is one bug, not two — the `.unknown` facet is only half-wired.** `buildPostings` emits an `.unknown` posting for exactly **five** dimensions — roaster, roasterCountry, originCountry, farm, profile (`CoffeeIndex.swift:464–490`) — and `matches(_:)` likewise only appends `.unknown` for those five (`:207–214`, via `keysWithUnknown`). The four **band** dimensions are handled at `:222–225` with their explicit band sets only and **never append `.unknown`**. Meanwhile `toggleFacet` has a wildcard `case (_, .unknown): flip(&filter.unknownDimensions, dimension)` (`FilterSheetView.swift:236`) that happily accepts `.unknown` for *any* dimension. So tapping the Price row sets `unknownDimensions = [.priceBand]`, **nothing reads it**, `priceBands` stays empty, no constraint is applied, and the listing shows all 411 coffees — exactly "priceless coffees lead to a list where some have prices". **This affects 3 of the 6 tappable rows**, not just price: `Rating` → `.ratingBand`, `Price` → `.priceBand`, `Altitude` → `.altitudeBand` are all dead the same way (`InsightsAggregation.swift:77–82`). **And the same dead path exists on the charts surface** — `InsightsView.selectInCoffees` routes through the same `toggleFacet`, so tapping the **Unknown slice** of any band chart (rating / price / €100g / altitude) silently shows everything too. **"Leads nowhere" is a second, separate defect:** `Weight` is filed with `dimension: nil` (`:84`) because there is no weight `FilterDimension`, so it renders as plain text with no `Button` — but it is visually **identical** to the tappable rows (same 44pt row, same layout), so it reads as a broken link rather than as static information. **Fix — (a) is the real one:** **(a)** emit `.unknown` postings for `ratingBand`/`priceBand`/`pricePer100gBand`/`altitudeBand` in `buildPostings`, and append `.unknown` in `matches(_:)` for those four the way `keysWithUnknown` already does for vocab dimensions. That repairs the data-quality rows **and** the chart Unknown slices in one change, and makes the four band Unknown buckets genuinely selectable in the filter sheet (which #47 intended). **(b)** add a `weight` dimension + `WeightBand` so the Weight row becomes tappable like the rest — Radu likely wants a weight facet anyway; if not, make non-interactive rows visually distinct (no chevron/tint) so they stop looking like dead links. **(c)** tighten `toggleFacet`'s wildcard: after (a) every dimension supports `.unknown`, so replace `case (_, .unknown)` with an explicit list — the wildcard is precisely why this rotted silently instead of failing loudly. **Acceptance:** tap `Price — N of 411 missing` and the listing shows exactly N coffees, all with no price; same for Rating and Altitude; the filter-state line reads `N of 411`; and the Unknown slice on the price chart agrees with it. Add a `CoffeeIndex` unit test per band dimension asserting the Unknown bucket's count equals the data-quality card's `missing` — the two numbers are computed by different code paths today and nothing checks that they agree. `Store/CoffeeIndex.swift`, `Features/Coffees/FilterSheetView.swift`; the weight facet and the row styling are ios-ux — claim in both. |

| 118 | backend | 8 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-06 (backend half), `a73b520`.** New `POST /api/coffees/quick-create {photoIds}`: `upsertCoffeeBase` (no LLM call) and returns `201 {id, reviewState}` immediately — `reviewState` is `'unextracted'`, the column's own pre-existing default (008_coffees.sql), so **no migration was needed**: a coffee nothing has extracted yet already looks different from one that has (`'needs_review'`/`'clean'`), which is exactly the "pending/extracting" signal #130/#131 need. The real extraction then runs *after* the response is sent, reusing the exact full-ensemble pipeline the daily batch worker uses (`defaultVoters()` + `processPhoto()` from `src/lib/worker.js`) rather than a new code path — so the result the review queue and snapshot sync see is indistinguishable from one the batch worker produced. Deliberately does **not** go through `runWorker()`/`claimBatch` — those take the global extraction advisory lock and would refuse to start (`already_running`) if the daily ingest job happened to be mid-run, silently stranding the new coffee at `unextracted` forever; `processPhoto()` alone takes no such lock. Guards the race the other way instead: the primary photo is leased (`extraction_leased_until`/`extraction_leased_by='quick-create'`) synchronously before the background pass fires, so the daily batch's own `claimBatch` (which excludes anything already leased) can't grab the same photo mid-run; `processPhoto()` clears that lease itself on completion. A background failure is caught in the route (never left to become an unhandled rejection that could crash the process) and increments `extraction_failures`/clears the lease, the same fields `runWorker`'s own per-photo catch updates — so a failed quick-create photo is retried by the next daily batch run exactly like any other worker failure, up to the same `extraction_failures` cutoff (#64). A second/back-of-bag photoId is marked `state='processed'` immediately (same fix #75's SAVE route already applies) since the async pipeline is one-photo-in like the batch worker itself — its image is never sent to a voter here, a real (unchanged) scope gap vs. the synchronous `/extract`+SAVE path, which does union every provided photoId's image. **Additive, not a replacement:** `POST /api/coffees/extract` and `POST /api/coffees` are both untouched, so the current synchronous wizard flow keeps working exactly as today until the iOS lanes below wire up the new endpoint — nothing forces the "drop confirm-before-save" product decision the original write-up flagged; iOS can choose to keep a lightweight confirm step that edits the pending coffee once it lands; **the confirm step it removed by this decision is only backend's synchronous /extract wait, not the iOS confirm SCREEN itself** — that stays possible against the review queue. 4 new tests (`coffees-extract.test.js`, same no-DB validation convention as `/extract`/`/evaluate`) — 315/315 `npm test` green. Live-verified: `GET /health` ok, `GET /api/admin/jobs` showed 0 running before push (safe per the hard rule), Railway redeploy green. **Not** live-exercised against production data (would create a real spurious coffee row from a live photo — no throwaway photoId existed to test against safely); the DB-touching path (upsert/lease/background-completion) is unverified beyond the code read and unit tests, same caveat as any route this repo's convention defers to "verify end-to-end against a real local Postgres" for, which no local Postgres was available here to do. Split into #130 (ios-shell) and #131 (ios-ux) for the client half, both unblocked now. `backend/src/routes/coffees.js` (+ test). — Original spec follows. **Add Coffee: extract in the BACKGROUND, don't block the app** (Radu, 2026-09-03 — "takes a looong time to extract… should happen in the background, not even sure if i need to keep app on and for how long"). Today the wizard calls `POST /api/coffees/extract` **synchronously** (`runLightExtraction` — a live rules+flash+reconciler ensemble), so the user waits on a slow free-tier LLM run before they can confirm/save, app foregrounded the whole time. Move it to the existing **async worker pipeline**: on submit, upload the photo(s)+text (already via `/api/photos/manifest`, #76), create the coffee immediately in a *pending/extracting* state, and **kick a fire-and-forget background extraction for just that photo server-side** (a scoped `runWorker` over the one photo, like `POST /api/admin/jobs` but auto-triggered on add) rather than waiting on `/extract`; the app returns instantly and the coffee fills in via the normal delta sync, with the review queue surfacing any uncertain fields. **Product decision for Radu:** this drops the wizard's "confirm every field before save" step in favour of "add now, review later in the queue" — confirm that's what you want (it's the natural way to make it background + no-keep-app-open). Cross-lane seam: **backend** (auto-kick background extraction on create + a pending state), **ios-shell** (submit-and-return flow, pending coffee in the store), **ios-ux** (wizard: submit dismisses immediately, a subtle "extracting…" indicator on the new row). `backend/src/routes/coffees.js`, `src/lib/worker.js`; `ios/.../Features/AddCoffee/**`, `Store/**`. |

| 160 | backend | 9 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09 — `POST /api/score` live on production and verified against the real corpus.** `{url, text}` → `{score, confidence, components, fields, missing, explanation, spentUsd, cached}`. **Deterministic-only**, per Radu's cost rule: `extractRuleFields` (the same free rules voter the batch worker runs first) and nothing else, so **no LLM fires on a page view and `spentUsd` is always 0**. Results cached 10 min by SHA-256 of the page text (not the URL — shop URLs carry cart and session params). `requireAnyToken`, not `requireIngestToken`: scoring is read-only and the extension should never carry the token that authorizes every write. **CORS turned out to need no server change at all** — an MV3 service-worker fetch covered by `host_permissions` is exempt, so `@fastify/cors` was never added; that only holds while #162 keeps its fetch in the service worker, which its own comments say loudly. **Three live results, real production data:** a Gardelli/Congo page → 45 (€7.40/100g, roasted 8d, recency 100); a Tim Wendelboe page with no price → headline correctly **suppressed**, affinity 75, and the explanation names the missing price as the reason; a 211-day-old Koppi → recency 0, score 33. **Two bugs found and fixed on the way, both pre-existing in #106's shipped endpoint:** (1) `parseProfile` returns a profile **slug** but `coffees.profile_id` is a SMALLINT FK, so `processStats.get("washed")` was always undefined — **the process signal (r=0.30, one of only four) has contributed nothing to any evaluation `/api/coffees/evaluate` has ever returned**, and it held the confidence gate down too. The persist path always mapped correctly (`worker.js:159`); the evaluate route did not. (2) novelty reported `isNewOrigin: true` for an origin it simply **failed to extract** — "we could not read it" and "it is new to you" are different facts, and the very first live call (a Congo coffee, #165) hit it. Now null, and the explanation stays silent. 341/341 tests green, 18 of them new. `backend/src/routes/score.js`, `backend/src/lib/{corpus,scoring}.js`, `backend/test/score.test.js`. |
| 161 | backend | 9 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09 — live on production and verified against the real library.** `/api/score` now also returns `match` (the coffee he already owns, or null) and `enrich` (fields the page has that the record lacks, or []). **Verified end to end:** a Gardelli Sopacdi shop page matched `fzh3Sz3vedFG4iGZms1FPA` — *"Cafea Gardelli Sopacdi (Congo) MAI 2018 aeropress", bought 2018-05-06, rated 4.0* — `confidence: strong`, `matchedOn: ["sopacdi"]`, and offered process, roast date, weight and price. **Matching is deliberately conservative**, because a false "you own this" invites writing page data onto the wrong record: the roaster must match exactly, and beyond that at least one *distinctive* word must be shared. Roaster name, country names (from the live vocab, so they track the seed), processes, generic coffee words, bare numbers and Romanian title words are all excluded — so "Gardelli Ethiopia Washed" matches nothing and two different Ethiopians from one roaster don't match each other. No shared distinctive word means **no match at all**, not a weak one; a disagreeing origin downgrades to `possible`. Runs in JS over the roaster's own coffees (414 across ~90 roasters) rather than SQL, so the whole decision is unit-testable without a DB. **Fill blanks only** — a field the record holds is never offered — and accepts route through #40's edit endpoint, which re-parses with the same parser the iOS edit sheet uses, refuses human-locked fields, and lands the result `decided_by='human'`. **Plausibility guards, added after the first live run tried to offer "9-2026 masl":** anything the parser flagged `needsReview` is dropped, altitude is bounded 100–3000 m, weight 20–5000 g, price > 0, roast date within 10 years (a future date is kept — pre-orders carry them). Root parser bug filed as #184. **Extension:** the owned card leads *above* the score, always states why it matched, and the enrich block stays hidden when the page adds nothing. Writes need `INGEST_TOKEN`, so options gained a **second, optional** write-token field — evaluate still works with a read token alone. **"Add as new" deliberately not built:** iOS stays how new bags are added (Radu, 2026-09-05). 375/375 tests green, 29 new, most of them pinning down what must NOT match. `backend/src/lib/enrich.js`, `backend/src/routes/score.js`, `extension/**`. |
| 162 | backend | 9 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09 — the extension is built and installable from `extension/`.** MV3, load-unpacked, install steps in `extension/README.md` (~2 min: `chrome://extensions` → Developer mode → Load unpacked → paste the **read** token in options). **Evaluate only** — "Add as new" and "Enrich existing" are #161's half and are not built. **Three design choices worth not undoing:** (1) **`activeTab` + `chrome.scripting`, not a content script** — the extension reads a page only after the toolbar button is clicked, so it needs no standing permission over every shop site Radu visits; `host_permissions` covers the API host alone. (2) **The API fetch lives in the service worker**, which is exactly what makes CORS a non-issue (see #160) — move it into the page and it breaks. (3) **No per-retailer parsers**, Radu's call: the scraper reads JSON-LD `Product` → OpenGraph → the **selected variant** → visible text and gathers generously; the server's deterministic pipeline parses. The variant matters more than it looks — grams live in the variant name, and with no grams there is no €/100g, which is what cost 5 of the 10 samples in `status/backend.md` their headline. The popup shows the headline with components at equal visual weight, names what the page failed to provide, and never calls the number a predicted rating (#106's wording, matching #125). Token lives in `chrome.storage.local`, entered by hand, never committed — repo is PUBLIC. `extension/**`, and `CLAUDE.md` §4 now lists `extension/**` under the backend lane. |

Live on production and confirmed through `/api/score`. **The affected coffee (`fzh3Sz3vedFG4iGZms1FPA`) now reads origin = Democratic Republic of the Congo, farm = Sopacdi**, both human-locked so no future pass can overwrite them. It has no photo, so it could never have been re-extracted, and `readjudicateAll` had no stored candidate to re-canonicalize — the edit path was the only route. **Two further bugs found and fixed on the way, both wider than Congo:** (1) **four countries were unmatchable for the same reason** — `findAliasMentions` reads `country_aliases` only, a country's own name is not implicitly an alias, and Cameroon (018), Hong Kong (021), Japan (023) and Greece (024) were all added without one. 034 ends with a self-heal driven off the schema, so it repairs those and any future repeat; CLAUDE.md §12 now documents the trap. (2) **editing a farm to a name not already in the vocab silently wrote NULL** — `resolveField` fired get-or-create on `!canonical`, but an unknown farm canonicalizes to a *truthy* `{id: null, name}` carrier (unlike a roaster, which returns null), so the branch never fired for the one vocab that is 0-seeded. Every farm ever typed into the edit sheet that wasn't already known was dropped with a 200 OK. Condition is now `canonical?.id == null`; the batch path was never affected. |

| 184 | data | 8 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09.** `parseAltitude` had two patterns and the LOOSER one ran first: a range whose unit was optional. So `2026-09-01` parsed as a 9–2026 m range, and beat an explicit `1750 masl` on the same page purely by appearing earlier in the string. **Now three patterns, tried in order of how much the text commits to being an altitude:** unit-anchored range → unit-anchored single → bare pair. A number with `masl` beside it always outranks one without, wherever each sits. The bare-pair branch requires **both endpoints ≥ 100 m** (coffee is never grown lower), which is what rejects `2026-09`, `09-2026` and the `01-09` of an ISO date, and it keeps scanning after a reject so a real bare range later in the text is still found. **Corpus impact, measured before changing anything: 2 of 297 stored altitudes had min < 100 m**, so this was mostly a forward fix. Of those two: `763QGOtkxKmjZ_ZzdZqgYA` (3–200) now parses to **null** and was cleared by re-adjudication; `bTGCBjHXyvEXbv_zovdOKg` (16–1700) is **not a parser bug** — its OCR text literally reads `16-1700 masl`, almost certainly a dropped `00` from `1600-1700`, and the parser already flags it `confidence 0.5 / needsReview`. Left as-is rather than guessed at; #161's enrich path drops anything `needsReview`, so it is never offered as a one-tap write. 12 new tests across #184/#185. `backend/src/lib/normalize.js`, `backend/test/normalize.test.js`. |
| 185 | data | 8 | done | — | **DONE 2026-09-09.** Added **RON, CZK, PLN, HUF, SEK, NOK, DKK** to `CURRENCY_PATTERNS`. The table carried symbols plus only EUR/USD/GBP/CHF, so `200 CZK`, `80 PLN` and `103 RON` parsed to an amount with a **null currency** and were dropped — invisible, because `canonicalize('price')` silently declines a price with no currency. Survivable while every price came from a photographed bag written in symbols, but JSON-LD's `Product.offers.priceCurrency` is an ISO code **by specification** and is the first thing the extension's scraper reads, so the value half of the score (0.50 of the headline) vanished on any Czech, Polish or Romanian shop. RON is Radu's most common currency — 103 of his coffees. **Fixed the way this row demanded: by recognising the codes, not by relaxing the "no currency, no price" rule**, since `resolveField` assumes `DEFAULT_PRICE_CURRENCY` (RON) for a bare amount and any loosening there would have turned `200 CZK` into `200 RON`. **Third bug found in the same table:** `ft` was the HUF marker under `/i`, so **`parsePrice('1500 ft')` — an altitude in FEET — returned 1500 HUF.** Hungarian capitalises the forint (`3500 Ft`); feet does not. That pattern is now case-sensitive (`Ft`/`FT`), while `huf` stays case-insensitive. Nordic codes are code-only on purpose: `kr` is shared by SEK/NOK/DKK and guessing between them is worse than declining. `backend/src/lib/normalize.js`, `backend/test/normalize.test.js`. |

A coffee already in the library is flagged **IN YOUR LIBRARY** (free, from #161's match). **Four decisions worth keeping:** (1) **pruning runs on every read and write, never on a timer** — an MV3 worker is evicted constantly and Chrome can be shut for a week, so a timer would make "discard after 10 days" depend on the browser having been open; a test covers a browser closed for a month. (2) **A revisit replaces its entry**, keyed on origin+path so cart ids, `utm_*` and variant params don't file one coffee under five — otherwise the ranking quietly becomes "pages I refreshed most". (3) **Unscored visits are saved but excluded from the ranking**, not sorted as zero: a suppressed headline is a deliberate outcome (#106), and ranking it below a genuinely bad coffee would misread it. They're counted at the bottom instead. (4) **The list renders even when the current page fails to score** — "always show" means the top 10 is reachable from any tab. **Storage is local, not backend:** no token, no round-trip, no schema, private to the machine — the cost is that it does not reach iOS, which is a separate row if Radu wants it. Page text is deliberately not stored (60 KB, worthless once scored). 406/406 tests green, 13 new, mostly on retention and revisit handling. `extension/**`. |

Migration: one `history` table `(token_hash, url_key, saved_at, payload jsonb)`, PK `(token_hash, url_key)`; token stored hashed so the DB never carries the bearer plaintext. Client keeps a small local cache for offline reads and races the network on popup open, mirroring how `getConfig`/`getScore` already behave. The score the popup renders becomes `displayScore = clamp(0, 100, score + adjustment)`, and the same `displayScore` is what the shortlist ranks on. **Storage shape:** extend the `historyKey`-keyed entry (`extension/history.js`) with an `adjustment: number` field (default 0), and add an `adjust(url, delta)` helper that upserts on top of the current entry. If #194 has shipped, the adjustment syncs across browsers via the server payload — same field, same shape. If not, it's per-browser like the rest of the pre-#194 shortlist. **UI:** the two buttons render only when a score exists (an unscored page has nothing to adjust); the current adjustment shows as a small badge `+20` / `-10` beside the number when non-zero, tap-and-hold or a small `↺` glyph resets. History rows show the *adjusted* number, with the raw score in the row's evaluator-note expand. **Not the same thing as `POST /api/coffees/:id/favorite`** — favorite is on owned coffees only and lives server-side; adjustment is on any page the extension has scored, mostly ones Radu does not own. **Version bump:** `extension/manifest.json` to 1.5.0 (feature bump, matching #189/#190's cadence). Tests: extend `history.js` tests for the adjust helper and the display-score clamp (0 and 100 boundaries). Not blocked on #194; ships whichever way. `extension/**`. |

So a coffee Radu keeps checking in on quietly resets its own clock and rides in the list forever, while one he saw once and shortlisted mentally falls off at exactly 10 days. Popup copy: `Coffees you evaluate are saved here for 30 days` and `${top.length} of ${total} · 30d`. **Server side (#194's `/api/history`)** is the authoritative timestamp per Radu's parenthetical — the server owns `addedAt`, the client's copy is a cache. A client seeing a row it doesn't have gets the server's `addedAt`; a client posting a new row gets `addedAt = server_now` in the response and stores that. Prune runs server-side on write, client-side on read (same belt-and-braces #187 already argues for). **Migration for existing installs:** on first load after the update, entries missing `addedAt` get it back-filled to `savedAt` — same as if he shortlisted them at their last visit, which underestimates by at most the current 10-day retention window. | 199 | backend | 10 | ready | — | **Extension: recompute both *roasted N days ago* and the *fit score* against today (Radu, 2026-09-12: verbatim, *'on extension make sure roasted x days ago gets updated based on today date'* — then, correcting an earlier draft of this row: *'stored score needs recompute since roasting date x days ago increases'*).** Today `entryFromScore` freezes `roastDays` and the blended `score` at the visit. Freezing was #188's design — keeping the chip colour and score in sync — but the *age* is a fact about the bag independent of the visit, and the *stale penalty* is a rule about that fact: a bag Radu shortlisted at 30d (no penalty) is 45d two weeks later (−10 penalty), and the shortlist number he'd rank against still reads the un-penalised score. Requires adding `value.score` and `novelty.score` to the stored entry (alongside the already-stored `affinity.score`, `roastedOn`, `pillCount`); today only `pillCount` and `affinity.score` land, so an older row has no way to re-blend. The blend is #189's weights `affinity 0.50 / roast 0.20 / value 0.15 / novelty 0.10`, renormalised over the components actually present (per #188 — a missing roast date renormalises, doesn't score zero), then `−10` if `days > 40`, clamped 0-100. **Port the same three constants — WEIGHTS, `STALE_ROAST_DAYS`, `STALE_ROAST_PENALTY` — to a new `extension/scoring-blend.js`, imported by both `backend/src/lib/scoring.js` and the extension**, so there is one definition of the blend and CLAUDE.md's public-contract rule can't drift between server and client on the next weight change (#188 → #189 already reweighted twice; the third time will not be the last). A contract test asserts server + extension blend produce identical numbers on a fixed fixture. **Older entries without the new component fields** fall back to the stored `score` (still frozen; the row degrades gracefully rather than showing nothing), and get the new fields when a revisit re-scores them. **What still stays frozen:** the raw component *inputs* (affinity/value/novelty), because they're snapshots of Radu's library at that moment — a roaster he later added to the library doesn't retroactively make that page's novelty score change, and re-fetching them would need the page text (deliberately not stored, per #187). Only the *roast* term recomputes. **Ranking uses the recomputed score** so a bag that just crossed 40 days visibly drops down the shortlist — otherwise the whole exercise is cosmetic. **Delete `entryFromScore`'s '#188: the age AS EVALUATED' comment block** — obsolete — and replace it with one paragraph explaining the score/age split: inputs frozen, roast term live. **Version bump:** `extension/manifest.json` to 1.5.0 (or the next feature digit after whichever of #196/#197/#198 lands first). Tests: (i) `scoring-blend.js` produces identical numbers to `backend/src/lib/scoring.js` on the existing scoring fixture (parity test), (ii) a fresh-visit-then-30-days-later render pulls both a new age *and* a re-blended score, (iii) a pre-migration entry (no `value.score`/`novelty.score`) falls back to the stored score without crashing. `extension/**`, plus one refactor extracting the blend from `backend/src/lib/scoring.js`. |

**Unblocking is a normal part of the job.** When you finish an item, flip every row
whose `needs` are now all `done` from `blocked` to `ready` in the same commit. If
you don't, the next lane has nothing to pick up.

**Running it surfaced a real bug**: `backfillOcrText` counted a row as
`updated` even when the bag photo was genuinely illegible and
`runOcrTranscribe` returned an empty transcription — `appendOcrTextToCoffee`
silently no-ops on empty text (by design, to avoid stamping an empty "OCR
text" heading), but the caller counted it as a success anyway. One specific
coffee hit this every single run — six consecutive `{"limit":15}` calls each
reported `{"scanned":1,"updated":1}` with an identical result, proof the
write never actually landed. Fixed `appendOcrTextToCoffee` to return whether
it wrote, and `backfillOcrText` to only count `updated` on a real write,
reporting `'OCR returned no legible text'` in `errors` otherwise (same
"broken row stays retryable, doesn't abort the sweep" convention the missing-
asset case already uses). Live-verified against a real local Postgres 16
with Gemini mocked at the `src/vertex.js` boundary. `npm test` 252/252 green.

Drained the production backlog down to exactly that one permanently-illegible
photo (everything else — dozens of coffees across ~15 small batches; a
`{"limit":50}` request reliably hit a transport-level connection drop under
this sandbox's proxy, so `{"limit":15}` or smaller was the reliable unit) —
the fix now correctly reports it as an `errors` entry instead of a false
`updated`. Flipped `#67` → `done`.

**Picked `#69` next** (lowest-numbered remaining `ready` backend row, phase
6, no `needs`) — the daily text-only OCR job could never reach an overdue
`awaiting_text` photo at all, because `claimBatch`'s SQL only included that
branch when the job's own `includeImages` was true. Made `claimBatch` always
claim overdue `awaiting_text` photos, and added `shouldUseImage(photo,
includeImages)` so `runWorker` decides image-vs-text-only **per claimed
photo** rather than per job — an `awaiting_text` row always gets its image
(no text will ever arrive), a `text_received` row still only gets its image
when the job asked for image mode. Live-verified against a real local
Postgres 16 (seeded overdue/not-yet-due/normal photos, confirmed `claimBatch`
claims the right pair and `shouldUseImage` routes each correctly). `npm test`
252/252 green (same run as #67's fix, both landed in one commit). Flipped
`#69` → `done`.

phase 6, no `needs`). Shipped `backfillOcrText({limit, spendCapUsd})` in
`src/lib/worker.js` + `POST /api/admin/backfill-ocr-text` in
`src/routes/admin.js`: a targeted re-OCR that re-runs only
`runOcrTranscribe` against each affected photo's stored `ocr` asset and appends
via the existing idempotent `appendOcrTextToCoffee` — no voters, no
re-adjudication, so the structured fields these coffees already have are never
disturbed. Scoped by SQL to exactly the affected population (image-only
`raw_caption IS NULL`, `state='processed'`, has an `ocr` asset, not already
carrying the `OCR text` heading), which also makes a partial run *resume*
instead of restart and makes stacking a second transcription impossible.
`npm test` **250/250 green**; verified three ways against a real local Postgres
16 with Gemini mocked at the `src/vertex.js` boundary ($0 spend): population
selection, spend-cap + resume (cap halted it at 1 of 5, uncapped re-run did
exactly the remaining 4), and error isolation (a missing asset file lands in
`errors` without aborting the sweep). **Not landed on `main`**: job 24 was
`running` and verified genuinely stuck (5 polls / 4 min, `photosDone` 0,
`spentUsd` 0, pinned to photo 269 on a Gemini 429 — the free-tier *daily*
quota is spent), and CLAUDE.md §12 forbids pushing `backend/**` while a job
runs. `POST /api/admin/jobs/24/pause` — the reversible fix the #61/#62 session
used for the identical situation — **was denied by this session's permission
classifier**, so per the #51 precedent the session stopped rather than working
around it, and specifically did *not* substitute the larger action (pushing
`backend/**`, which auto-deploys and SIGTERMs the worker) that the pause was
meant to make safe. Only `status/**` went to `main` (no `backend/**` path
match → no deploy). Row left **`claimed`**, since `done` means "on the shared
branch". **Correction recorded on a final re-check at 07:02 UTC: job 24 was
NOT wedged** — it read `photosDone` 12 / `spentUsd` $0.0175, up from 0/0. The
429 was Gemini's *per-minute* rate limit with a ~59 s retry-after throttling
throughput, not the daily cap being spent; a 4-minute poll window was too short
to see it move. So the hold was right for a stronger reason than the session
thought (a push would have SIGTERM'd a worker doing real paid work), and the
**pause would have been wrong too** — the denial prevented actual harm, not
just a procedural violation. Lesson for the next session: `photosDone 0` +
`spentUsd 0` + a repeated `lastError` photo id is **not** proof of a wedged job
when the error is a 429 with a retry-after; watch over tens of minutes, or use
`spentUsd` creeping up as the tell, and don't equate it with jobs 14/15's
genuinely-wedged non-retryable 403. **Let job 24 finish on its own; do not
pause it.** Exact 4-step finish sequence is in `status/backend.md`; note the
production backfill itself **costs real flash-lite calls (~1 per coffee, ~95
coffees)**, so it must be run bounded (`{"limit":50}`) across days, unlike this
lane's earlier $0 backfills.

**The product brief lives in the OTHER repo.** `Climb-Again/mycoffee-private` is
attached to every lane routine as a second source; the brief is
`brief/MyCoffee app.docx` on its `main`. That is the **authoritative source for the
roaster and country vocabularies** (#12) — `PLAN.md` carries the analysis and the
alias pairs but *not* all ~105 roaster names verbatim, so seeding from `PLAN.md`
alone would produce a silently incomplete vocabulary. Extract it with
`unzip` + parse `word/document.xml` (`<w:p>` paragraphs, `<w:t>` runs); note that
many entries split the name and its `(count)` across **adjacent paragraphs**, so
pair them when parsing.

**Never copy the docx or any personally-identifying excerpt into this public repo.**
Extracted vocabulary *values* are the deliverable; the document is not. It was moved
out precisely because it holds ten years of personal purchase history and
screenshots containing a profile avatar.

**Frankfurter is reachable and verified (#34 unblocked).** Both
`api.frankfurter.app` and `api.frankfurter.dev` are allowlisted. `.app`
301-redirects to `.dev/v1/…`, so **follow redirects** (`curl -L` / `fetch` default)
or call `.dev` directly.

Working request shape, confirmed against a live response — do not guess the params:

```
GET https://api.frankfurter.dev/v1/2015-01-01..2015-01-31?base=EUR&symbols=RON
→ {"amount":1.0,"base":"EUR","start_date":…,"end_date":…,"rates":{"2015-01-02":{"RON":4.4761}, …}}
```

Note it is **`base` + `symbols`** (not `from`/`to`), the path is `/v1/`-prefixed, and
`rates` is keyed by date with a nested currency object. Only ECB business days are
present — expect ~21–22 observations per month, which is the correct set to average.

**Verified inversion anchors.** Frankfurter returns EUR→X; `fx_rates.rate_to_eur`
is "1 unit of quote = N EUR", so invert. Real values:

| Month | EUR→RON | `rate_to_eur` |
|---|---|---|
| 2015-01 | 4.4872 | **0.222856** |
| 2019-06 | 4.7259 | 0.211602 |
| 2024-06 | 4.9767 | **0.200935** |

If a 2015 RON row comes out near `4.49` instead of `0.22`, the direction is
backwards and every RON price will be ~24× too large. Use these three as the test
fixture. RON drifted 4.49 → 5.23 (~17%) across the corpus window, which is exactly
why the rate must be dated rather than flat.

## Spend gates (data lane)

Radu's explicit instruction, in order:
1. Deterministic rules pass over the corpus — **free**, run freely.
2. **5 photos only** (~$0.35) → **stop and report every extracted field** so Radu
   can judge accuracy himself.
3. Only after he approves: 25-record tuning run, then re-adjudicate for $0 until
   thresholds settle.
4. **Never launch the full ~$62 backfill autonomously.** Prepare, set
   `EXTRACTION_MAX_SPEND_USD=80`, then ask.

Confirm current Vertex per-token rates before any LLM run — the figures in
`PLAN.md` §2 are estimates, and Gemini 2.5's thinking tokens dominate output cost.
